# Sessions

Ported from `.references/pi/packages/coding-agent/docs/sessions.md` at pin
`8fa7eebd`. Claims below are bound to the evidence manifest
[evidence/sessions.json](evidence/sessions.json); anything not yet provable
in the Rust port is listed under "Pending port surface" instead of being
described as working.

## Session storage

Sessions auto-save as JSONL transcript files under the session directory. The
directory is selected by `--session-dir`, by the `PI_CODING_AGENT_SESSION_DIR`
environment variable, or defaults under the agent config directory
(`PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR`, default `~/.pi/agent`). Both environment variables are
documented in the executed `--help` snapshot:

```text
pi - AI coding assistant with read, bash, edit, write tools

Usage:
  pi [options] [@files...] [messages...]

Commands:
  pi install <source> [-l]     Install extension source and add to settings
<!-- doc-c:fence=sessions.01 source=target/verification/docs-topics/cli-help/pi--help.txt -->
```

Each session file holds a tree of entries; the record shapes and the v1/v2/v3
format lineage are documented in [session-format.md](session-format.md). The
`--help` examples block shows the on-disk layout that `--export` accepts:

```text
pi - AI coding assistant with read, bash, edit, write tools

Usage:
  pi [options] [@files...] [messages...]

Commands:
  pi install <source> [-l]     Install extension source and add to settings
  pi remove <source> [-l]      Remove extension source from settings
  pi uninstall <source> [-l]   Alias for remove
<!-- doc-c:fence=sessions.02 source=target/verification/docs-topics/cli-help/pi--help.txt -->
```

## Session flags

The session-related options accepted by this build, quoted verbatim from the
executed `--help` snapshot:

```text
pi - AI coding assistant with read, bash, edit, write tools

Usage:
  pi [options] [@files...] [messages...]

Commands:
  pi install <source> [-l]     Install extension source and add to settings
  pi remove <source> [-l]      Remove extension source from settings
  pi uninstall <source> [-l]   Alias for remove
  pi update [source|self|pi]   Update pi, extensions, or model catalogs
  pi list                      List installed extensions from settings
  pi config [-l]               Open TUI to enable/disable package resources (Tab switches scope)
  pi <command> --help          Show help for install/remove/uninstall/update/list/config

Options:
  --provider <name>              Provider name (default: google)
  --model <pattern>              Model pattern or ID (supports "provider/id" and optional ":<thinking>")
  --api-key <key>                API key (defaults to env vars)
  --system-prompt <text>         System prompt (default: coding assistant prompt)
  --append-system-prompt <text>  Append text or file contents to the system prompt (can be used multiple times)
  --mode <mode>                  Output mode: text (default), json, or rpc
  --print, -p                    Non-interactive mode: process prompt and exit
<!-- doc-c:fence=sessions.03 source=target/verification/docs-topics/cli-help/pi--help.txt -->
```

`--continue`/`-c` reopens the previous session, `--resume`/`-r` selects a
session to resume, `--session` opens a specific session file or partial UUID,
and `--session-id` targets an exact project session ID, creating it when
missing. `--no-session` runs without saving a session file, and `--name`/`-n`
sets the display name recorded with the session. See
[usage.md](usage.md) for the full option surface.

## Forking a session

`--fork <path|id>` copies a session into a new session file. The e2e-smoke
run exercised this against the real binary by launching pi with
`--session-dir` and `--fork`:

```ts
import {
	copyFileSync,
	existsSync,
	mkdirSync,
	mkdtempSync,
	readdirSync,
	readFileSync,
	statSync,
	writeFileSync,
} from "node:fs";
import { createHash } from "node:crypto";
import { basename, join, relative, resolve } from "node:path";
import {
<!-- doc-c:fence=sessions.04 source=scripts/verification/e2e-smoke.ts -->
```

The forked session produced by that run carries a v3 header whose
`parentSession` field records the source session file, and the entry tree
(including ids and parent ids) is preserved in the new file:

```text

```

The same run then reopened the forked file with `--session` and confirmed the
reload preserved the session bytes, and the pinned TypeScript CLI reopened the
identical file afterwards (see [compaction.md](compaction.md) for the
byte-stability evidence and [evidence/sessions.json](evidence/sessions.json)
for the transcript binding).

## Session interoperability

Session files written by this port are reopened by the pinned TypeScript
reference `SessionManager` in the session-interop verification run: the Rust
session layer processed fixtures across the v1/v2/v3 lineage, and the
TypeScript implementation reopened every produced file (the manifest binds the
produced `continued.jsonl` transcript). Record shapes and the migration rules
are covered in [session-format.md](session-format.md).

## Exporting a session

A session file can be exported to HTML without entering the interactive UI:

```text
pi - AI coding assistant with read, bash, edit, write tools

Usage:
  pi [options] [@files...] [messages...]

Commands:
  pi install <source> [-l]     Install extension source and add to settings
  pi remove <source> [-l]      Remove extension source from settings
  pi uninstall <source> [-l]   Alias for remove
  pi update [source|self|pi]   Update pi, extensions, or model catalogs
  pi list                      List installed extensions from settings
<!-- doc-c:fence=sessions.06 source=target/verification/docs-topics/cli-help/pi--help.txt -->
```

## Pending port surface

- interactive `/resume` session picker (search, delete, `trash` CLI deletion)
  behind `--resume`/`-r` — unported-feature
- `/tree` tree navigation and in-place branching UI — unported-feature
- `/fork`, `/clone`, `/new`, `/name`, `/session`, `/share`, and `/export`
  slash commands — unported-feature
- branch summaries generated when switching branches — unported-feature
